\item $u_n\to u$ strongly in $L^3(U;\R^3)$;
\item $Du_n\rightharpoonup Du$ weakly in $L^2(U;\R^{3\times3})$;
\item $p_n\to p$ strongly in $L^{3/2}(U)$.
\end{enumerate}
Then $(u,Du,p,0)$ is a suitable weak solution on $\Omega\times I$ in the
sense of Definition~\ref{def:sws}. In particular, the almost-every-time
weak-gradient clause for $Du$ follows from (ii) and (iii); it is not an
additional hypothesis.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fix a local box $U=\Omega'\times J$. The fields $u,Du,p$ are already
global in the theorem statement, and their restrictions to each such box are
the same fixed fields. The strong $L^3$ convergence, weak $L^2$ convergence,
and strong $L^{3/2}$ convergence identify these restrictions with measurable
equivalence classes on $U$. A countable cover of $\Omega\times I$ therefore
verifies global measurability of the given fields; no separately chosen local
versions need to be patched on overlaps. The zero force is measurable and
belongs to every required local $L^q$ space.

The local energy of $u$ is finite because $u\in L^3(U)$ and $U$ has finite
measure. Weak lower semicontinuity gives $Du\in L^2(U)$ and
\[
 \int_J\int_{\Omega'}\abs{Du}^2\,dx\,dt
 \leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}
       \int_J\int_{\Omega'}\abs{Du_n}^2\,dx\,dt.
\]
Also $p\in L^{3/2}(U)$ by (iv). We prove the slice essential-supremum bound
separately, since it does not follow by applying space-time lower
semicontinuity to the essential supremum. Put
\[
 M=\sup_n\esssup_{t\in J}\int_{\Omega'}\abs{u_n(x,t)}^2\,dx<\infty.
\]
By (ii), choose a subsequence $(n_k)$ such that
$\sum_k\norm{u_{n_k}-u}_{L^3(U)}^3<\infty$. Tonelli's theorem then implies
that $u_{n_k}(\cdot,t)\to u(\cdot,t)$ in $L^3(\Omega')$ for almost every
$t\in J$. Since $\Omega'$ has finite measure, Hölder's inequality gives
\[
 \norm{g}_{L^2(\Omega')}
       \leq |\Omega'|^{1/6}\norm{g}_{L^3(\Omega')},
\]
so the same subsequence converges in $L^2(\Omega')$ for almost every such
$t$. For each $k$, the bound defining $M$ holds outside a null set of times;
the union of these countably many null sets is null. Passing to the slice
limit outside that union yields
\[
 \esssup_{t\in J}\int_{\Omega'}\abs{u(x,t)}^2\,dx\leq M.
\]
This proves all the integrability and energy clauses of (S1), except its
weak-gradient clause.

We derive that clause from (ii) and (iii). Fix components $i,j$, a function
$\xi\in C_c^\infty(\Omega')$, and $\eta\in C_c^\infty(\operatorname{int}J)$.
For each $n$, the slice weak-gradient property in (S1) and Fubini give
\[
 \int_J\eta(t)\int_{\Omega'}
       \bigl(u_{n,i}(x,t)\partial_j\xi(x)
              +(Du_n)_{ij}(x,t)\xi(x)\bigr)\,dx\,dt=0.
\]
The first term passes to the limit by strong $L^3$ convergence, and the
second by weak $L^2$ convergence tested against $\eta(t)\xi(x)$, which is in
$L^2(U)$. Thus for each fixed $\xi$, the function
\[
 G_{ij,\xi}(t)=\int_{\Omega'}
       \bigl(u_i(x,t)\partial_j\xi(x)+Du_{ij}(x,t)\xi(x)\bigr)\,dx
\]
has zero pairing with every such $\eta$, and hence vanishes for almost every
$t\in J$. To obtain one exceptional set for all spatial tests, choose
compact sets $K_1\subset K_2\subset\cdots$ with $K_m\subset\operatorname{int}K_{m+1}$,
$K_{m+1}\subset\Omega'$ and $\bigcup_mK_m=\Omega'$. For each $m$ choose a
countable family $\mathcal F_m$ of smooth functions supported in $K_{m+1}$
that is dense, in the $C^1$ norm, among the smooth functions supported in
$K_{m+1}$; every test supported in $K_m$ is then a $C^1$ limit of members of
$\mathcal F_m$, all supported in the fixed compact set $K_{m+1}$. Let $N$ be
the union of the null sets of times for the countable family
$\bigcup_m\mathcal F_m$. For $t\notin N$ such that $u(\cdot,t)$ and
$Du(\cdot,t)$ are locally integrable, the displayed pairing is continuous
under $C^1$ convergence with supports in $K_{m+1}$, so the identity extends
from $\mathcal F_m$ to every $\xi\in\Cc(\Omega')$ supported in $K_m$, and
every test is supported in some $K_m$.
The weak-gradient identity therefore holds for every spatial test function
for almost every $t\in J$. This is exactly the remaining clause of (S1).

For (S2), take $\psi\in\Cc(\Omega\times I)$. Its support is compact, so it
lies in a local box, and (ii) implies convergence of
$\iint u_n\cdot\nabla\psi$ to $\iint u\cdot\nabla\psi$. The former integral
is zero for every $n$; hence the limit is zero.

For (S3), fix $\varphi\in\Cc(\Omega\times I;\R^3)$ and choose a local box
containing its support. Strong $L^3$ convergence passes the linear velocity
term in $L^1$. It also passes the quadratic term because
\[
 \norm{u_n\otimes u_n-u\otimes u}_{L^{3/2}}
 \leq(\norm{u_n}_{L^3}+\norm{u}_{L^3})\norm{u_n-u}_{L^3}\to0.
\]
The weak gradient term passes by (iii), since $\nabla\varphi\in L^2$.
Finally, (iv) passes the pressure term in $L^1$ against the bounded
$\operatorname{div}\varphi$. Each $u_n$ satisfies (S3) with force zero, so
the limiting identity is (S3) for $(u,Du,p,0)$.

For (S4), fix a nonnegative $\psi\in\Cc(\Omega\times I)$ and take a local
box $U$ containing its support. Multiplication by the bounded function
$\sqrt\psi$ preserves weak convergence in $L^2(U)$, so
$\sqrt\psi Du_n\rightharpoonup\sqrt\psi Du$. Hilbert-space lower
semicontinuity yields
\[
 \int_U\abs{Du}^2\psi\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}
                         \int_U\abs{Du_n}^2\psi.
\]
On the right side of the local energy inequality, (ii) gives
$\abs{u_n}^2\to\abs u^2$ in $L^{3/2}(U)$. The cubic flux converges in $L^1$:
\[
 \norm{\abs{u_n}^2u_n-\abs u^2u}_{L^1(U)}
 \leq C(\norm{u_n}_{L^3(U)}^2+\norm{u}_{L^3(U)}^2)
                   \norm{u_n-u}_{L^3(U)}\to0.
\]
The pressure flux also converges in $L^1$, since
\[
 \norm{p_nu_n-pu}_{L^1(U)}
 \leq\norm{p_n-p}_{L^{3/2}(U)}\norm{u_n}_{L^3(U)}
      +\norm p_{L^{3/2}(U)}\norm{u_n-u}_{L^3(U)}\to0.
\]
All derivatives of $\psi$ are bounded and supported in $U$, so these
convergences show that the right side of (S4) for $u_n,p_n$ tends to its
right side for $u,p$. Taking the lower limit in the suitable inequality for
each $n$ proves (S4) for $(u,Du,p,0)$. Thus every clause (S1)--(S4) holds.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Restriction of suitable weak solutions]\label{lem:sws-restrict}
Let $q>\nfs52$, let $\Omega\subset\R^3$ be open, let $I\subset\R$ be an open
interval, and suppose $(u,Du,p,f)$ is a suitable weak solution on
$\Omega\times I$ in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:sws}. If
$\Omega_1\subset\Omega$ is open and $I_1\subset I$ is an open interval,
then the restrictions of $(u,Du,p,f)$ form a suitable weak solution on
$\Omega_1\times I_1$ with the same exponent $q$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $\Omega'\subset\Omega_1$ be open with compact closure in $\Omega_1$ and
let $J$ be an interval with compact closure in $I_1$. Then
$\overline{\Omega'}\subset\Omega$ and $\overline J\subset I$, so
$\Omega'\times J$ is also a local box for $\Omega\times I$. Every local
measurability, integrability, essential-supremum, and slice weak-gradient
clause in (S1) therefore restricts to this box. This proves (S1) for the
smaller domain, including local $L^q$ membership of $f$.

The test functions in (S2)--(S4) are globally defined smooth functions with
compact support contained in the relevant space-time domain. A test function
supported in $\Omega_1\times I_1$ is consequently also an admissible test
function for $\Omega\times I$. Its derivatives vanish outside its support,
so every integrand in (S2) and (S3), and every term in (S4), has the same
integral over $\Omega_1\times I_1$ as over $\Omega\times I$. The original
identities and inequality therefore give (S2)--(S4) on the smaller domain.
\end{proof}

\section{The limit is a suitable Leray--Hopf solution}\label{sec:limit}

Spatial tails turn the local compactness of the regularized velocities into
convergence on the whole space. The weak $L^2$ limit of each time slice is
retained, because the Leray--Hopf energy inequality is required at every time.
The dissipation passes through weak lower semicontinuity in $L^2$; no
almost-everywhere convergence of the gradients is used.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:leray-limit}
Let $a\in\J$, and let $\varepsilon_n\in(0,1]$ tend to zero. There are a
subsequence, still denoted $\varepsilon_n$, and measurable fields
$u\colon\R^3\times[0,\infty)\to\R^3$ and
$Du\colon\R^3\times(0,\infty)\to\R^{3\times3}$ with the following
properties. Choose a measurable representative of $a$ and set
$u(x,0)=a(x)$. For $t>0$, take the jointly measurable representative given,
componentwise on each finite slab by Lemma~\ref{lem:compactness}, using its
fixed normalized bump profile $\rho$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leray-limit-representative}
u_i(x,t)=
\begin{cases}
\displaystyle\limsup_{m\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}
   \bigl(u_{\varepsilon_n}(\cdot,t)*\rho_{1/m}\bigr)_i(x),
   &\text{if this value is finite},\\
0,&\text{otherwise},
\end{cases}
\qquad i=1,2,3\,.
\end{equation}
The diagonal subsequence can be chosen so that for every $T>0$,
\begin{align}
 u_{\varepsilon_n}&\longrightarrow u
   &&\text{strongly in }L^2(\R^3\times(0,T)),\label{eq:leray-limit-L2}\\
 Du_{\varepsilon_n}&\rightharpoonup Du
   &&\text{weakly in }L^2(\R^3\times(0,T)),\label{eq:leray-limit-Du}\\
 u_{\varepsilon_n}&\longrightarrow u
   &&\text{strongly in }L^q(\R^3\times(0,T))
       \quad\text{for every }2\le q<10/3,\label{eq:leray-limit-Lq}\\
 J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n}&\longrightarrow u
   &&\text{strongly in }L^3(\R^3\times(0,T)).\label{eq:leray-limit-JL3}
\end{align}
For every $t>0$, the slices $u_{\varepsilon_n}(\cdot,t)$ converge weakly to
$u(\cdot,t)$ in $L^2(\R^3)$, and $Du(\cdot,t)$ is the weak spatial
gradient of $u(\cdot,t)$ for almost every $t>0$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:compactness} to $u_{\varepsilon}$ on
$\R^3\times(0,m)$ for each positive integer $m$, and take a diagonal
subsequence. The uniform slice and gradient bounds required there follow
from Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-energy}; the translated-test time modulus is
Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-equicontinuity}. The construction in that lemma gives
\eqref{eq:leray-limit-representative}, local strong $L^2$ convergence, and
local weak convergence of the gradients. To track the representative, write
the first diagonal subsequence as $\varepsilon_{\sigma(n)}$. If its mollified
limit is taken along the same extraction once more, the resulting indices are
$\sigma(\sigma(n))$. For each fixed spatial mollification scale, the inner
pairings already converge along $\varepsilon_{\sigma(n)}$, so this further
restriction leaves their limits unchanged. Thus the iterated representative
in \eqref{eq:leray-limit-representative} is the same one selected by the
compactness argument. On each finite slab the global energy bound also makes
the gradients bounded in $L^2$. A further diagonal weak extraction on the
integer slabs therefore gives global weak convergence in
\eqref{eq:leray-limit-Du}; its limit agrees with the local weak gradient by
uniqueness of distributional limits and does not change the velocity
representative.

We next exhaust the exterior region uniformly in time and in the
regularization parameter. Fix $T>0$ and an integer $n\ge1$, and set
$R_1=n+1$ and $R_2=2(n+1)$. For $0<\varepsilon\le1$, positivity and support
of the mollifier, Jensen's inequality, and Fubini give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leray-initial-tail}
 \int_{\abs{x}>R_1}\abs{J_\varepsilon a(x)}^2\,dx
 \le \int_{\abs{y}>R_1-1}\abs{a(y)}^2\,dy\,.
\end{equation}
Since $R_1-1=n$, Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-tails} now yields, for every $t\in[0,T]$ and all $0<\varepsilon\le1$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leray-uniform-tail}
\begin{aligned}
 \int_{\abs{x}>2(n+1)}\abs{u_\varepsilon(x,t)}^2\,dx
 &\le \int_{\abs{y}>n}\abs{a(y)}^2\,dy\\
 &\quad+C\frac{\norm{a}_2^2T^{1/2}+\norm{a}_2^3T^{1/4}}{n+1}
 =:\eta_n(T)\,.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Both terms in $\eta_n(T)$ tend to zero as $n\to\infty$. Local weak convergence
on every time slice and lower semicontinuity on bounded annuli imply, for each
$S>2(n+1)$ and $t\in(0,T)$,
\begin{equation*}
 \int_{2(n+1)<\abs{x}<S}\abs{u(x,t)}^2\,dx\le\eta_n(T)\,.
\end{equation*}
Letting $S\to\infty$ by monotone convergence gives the same tail bound for
$u(\cdot,t)$. Thus the $L^2$ tails of both $u_\varepsilon$ and $u$ on
$\R^3\times(0,T)$ are uniformly small outside sufficiently large balls.

On each $\R^3\times(\delta,T)$, spatial tail control and the local strong
convergence from Lemma~\ref{lem:compactness} give strong $L^2$ convergence.
The initial strip is controlled by the uniform energy bound:
\begin{equation*}
 \int_0^\delta\int_{\R^3}\abs{u_\varepsilon}^2\,dx\,dt
 \le \delta\norm{a}_2^2\,.
\end{equation*}
A subsequence converges almost everywhere on $\R^3\times(0,T)$ by local
strong convergence on a countable exhaustion of the open slab. Fatou's
lemma gives the same upper bound for $u$ on $(0,\delta)$. Thus
$\norm{u_\varepsilon-u}_{L^2(\R^3\times(0,\delta))}^2
\le4\delta\norm{a}_2^2$. Letting $\delta\downarrow0$ proves strong
convergence on the full slab and hence \eqref{eq:leray-limit-L2}.

Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-ten-thirds} gives a uniform $L^{10/3}$ bound on every
finite slab, and Fatou's lemma gives the same bound for $u$. Interpolating
this bound with \eqref{eq:leray-limit-L2} proves
\eqref{eq:leray-limit-Lq} for $2\le q<10/3$. In particular,
$u_{\varepsilon_n}\to u$ in $L^3$. Since spatial convolution is a
contraction on $L^3$ and $J_{\varepsilon_n}u\to u$ in
$L^3(\R^3\times(0,T))$,
\begin{equation*}
\norm{J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}
\le \norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}
 +\norm{J_{\varepsilon_n}u-u}_{L^3}\longrightarrow0\,.
\end{equation*}
The approximate-identity convergence follows first for finite sums of
smooth compactly supported spatial functions times time indicators and then
for all $L^3$ fields by density and contraction.

It remains to strengthen the slice convergence to global $L^2$. The tail
bound just proved makes the tails uniformly small for $u_{\varepsilon_n}(t)$
and $u(t)$ on every fixed slab, while Lemma~\ref{lem:compactness} gives
weak convergence in $L^2$ on each bounded spatial set for every $t>0$.
Approximating a global $L^2$ test by its restriction to a large ball proves
$u_{\varepsilon_n}(\cdot,t)\rightharpoonup u(\cdot,t)$ in global $L^2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:leray-hopf-limit}
For the fields in Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-limit}, and every $T>0$,
$(u,Du)$ is a Leray--Hopf solution on $\R^3\times[0,T]$ with initial datum
$a$ in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:leray-hopf}; that is, it satisfies
(LH1)--(LH5).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The local weak-gradient conclusion and measurability in (LH1) follow from
Lemma~\ref{lem:compactness}; the global $L^2$ bounds and the global
$L^2$-integrability of $Du$ follow from Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-energy} and
weak lower semicontinuity. For almost every $t$, the weak spatial gradient
is therefore $Du(\cdot,t)$. Since every $u_{\varepsilon_n}(\cdot,t)$ is
divergence-free, local weak convergence on each slice gives
$\int u(x,t)\cdot\nabla\psi(x)\,dx=0$ for every $\psi\in C_c^\infty(\R^3)$
and every $t>0$.

For $w\in C_c^\infty(\R^3;\R^3)$, Lemma~\ref{lem:compactness} gives
continuity of $t\mapsto\int u(x,t)\cdot w(x)\,dx$ on $(0,T)$. The
initial value follows from the uniform modulus in
Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-equicontinuity}: if $\omega_w(h)\to0$ is its bound,
then for every $t>0$ the global weak slice convergence and
$J_{\varepsilon_n}a\to a$ in $L^2$ give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leray-weak-initial}
 \abs{\int (u(x,t)-a(x))\cdot w(x)\,dx}\le\omega_w(t)\,.
\end{equation}
The convergence $J_{\varepsilon_n}a\to a$ follows from the approximate
identity property in $L^2$, proved by density of smooth compactly supported
fields and $L^2$ contraction. The energy bound gives
$\sup_{t\ge0}
\norm{u(t)}_2\le
\norm{a}_2$. Approximating an arbitrary
$w\in L^2(\R^3;\R^3)$ by smooth compactly supported fields therefore
extends both the continuity and the limit at zero to every such $w$.
Applying the compactness lemma on a slightly larger time interval also
covers the right endpoint $T$. This proves (LH2) on $[0,T]$.

For the weak equation, use Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-momentum} with a
solenoidal test $\varphi\in C_c^\infty(\R^3\times(0,T);\R^3)$. The linear
terms pass by strong $L^2$ convergence of $u_{\varepsilon_n}$, weak $L^2$
convergence of $Du_{\varepsilon_n}$, and compact support of the test. The
transport tensors converge in $L^{3/2}$ because
$J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n}\to u$ and
$u_{\varepsilon_n}\to u$ in $L^3$. Thus the limit is the weak identity
(LH3); the pressure term vanishes since $\dv\varphi=0$.

Fix $t_0\in(0,T]$. The global weak slice convergence in
Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-limit} gives weak $L^2$ convergence at $t_0$,
and the global weak gradient convergence restricts to
$\R^3\times(0,t_0)$. Lower semicontinuity of the two Hilbert norms, applied
to the energy equality (R5), gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leray-energy-limit}
 \frac12\norm{u(t_0)}_2^2+\int_0^{t_0}\int_{\R^3}\abs{Du}^2\,dx\,dt
 \le\liminf_{n\to\infty}\left(
 \frac12\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}(t_0)}_2^2
 +\int_0^{t_0}\int_{\R^3}\abs{\nabla u_{\varepsilon_n}}^2\,dx\,dt\right)
 =\frac12\norm{a}_2^2\,.
\end{equation}
Here the equality on the right uses $J_{\varepsilon_n}a\to a$ in $L^2$.
This is exactly (LH4) at $t_0$. At $t_0=0$, $u(0)=a$ gives equality in
(LH4).

Finally, (LH2) implies $u(t)\rightharpoonup a$ in $L^2$ as $t\downarrow0$,
and (LH4) gives $\limsup_{t\downarrow0}\norm{u(t)}_2\le\norm{a}_2$.
The Hilbert-space identity $\norm{u(t)-a}_2^2=\norm{u(t)}_2^2+
\norm{a}_2^2-2\langle u(t),a\rangle$
then proves (LH5). In particular, weak lower semicontinuity in
$L^2((0,t_0);L^2)$, rather than Fatou's lemma, supplies the dissipation
term in (LH4).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:leray-pressure-limit}
For the fields in Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-limit}, define
$p=P[u\otimes u]$ by Definition~\ref{def:riesz-pressure}. For every $T>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leray-pressure-L32}
 p_{\varepsilon_n}\longrightarrow p
 \qquad\text{strongly in }L^{3/2}(\R^3\times(0,T))\,.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By (R4), $p_{\varepsilon_n}=P[F_n]$ for
$(F_n)_{ij}=(J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n})_i
(u_{\varepsilon_n})_j$. The strong $L^3$ convergences in
Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-limit} and H\"older's inequality give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leray-pressure-product}
\norm{F_n-u\otimes u}_{L^{3/2}}
 \le 
\norm{J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}
\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}}_{L^3}
       +
\norm{u}_{L^3}
\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}
 \longrightarrow0\,.
\end{equation}
The pressure operator in Definition~\ref{def:riesz-pressure} is bounded on
$L^{3/2}$, so applying it to \eqref{eq:leray-pressure-product} proves
\eqref{eq:leray-pressure-L32}. This also identifies the limit with the
canonical pressure, without a time-dependent additive constant.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:leray}]
Fix $a\in\J$ and take the sequence of regularized solutions with
$\varepsilon_n\to0$. Propositions~\ref{prop:leray-limit}--
\ref{prop:leray-pressure-limit} give a global Leray--Hopf pair $(u,Du)$ and
the pressure $p=P[u\otimes u]$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:u-ten-thirds},
$u\otimes u\in L^{5/3}(\R^3\times(0,T))$; the bounded $L^{5/3}$ extension
in Definition~\ref{def:riesz-pressure} therefore gives
$p\in L^{5/3}(\R^3\times(0,T))$. This is the same canonical pressure used
in Theorem~\ref{thm:assoc-pressure}. It remains to verify that the limit is
suitable in the precise sense of Definition~\ref{def:sws}.

The regularity and weak-gradient passages in clause (S1), and the weighted
weak lower semicontinuity used in (S4), are the corresponding clausewise
arguments from the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:stability}. The approximating
pairs here do not satisfy the unregularized Navier--Stokes equations: their
transport field is $J_\varepsilon u_\varepsilon$. Thus Theorem~
\ref{thm:stability} does not apply as a theorem to these pairs. We pass
instead to the limit directly in Lemmas~\ref{lem:reg-local-energy} and~\ref{lem:reg-momentum}, which are the identities
proved for the regularized pairs.

For (S1), measurability and the slice weak-gradient property follow from
Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-limit}, and (LH1) supplies the local energy
bounds. The pressure belongs to $L^{3/2}$ on every finite slab by
Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-pressure-limit}. For (S2), the divergence-free
identity was proved in (LH1).

Let $\varphi\in C_c^\infty(\R^3\times(0,T);\R^3)$. Passing to the limit
in Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-momentum} is justified by the linear convergences in Proposition~
\ref{prop:leray-limit}, the strong pressure convergence in
Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-pressure-limit}, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leray-NS-transport-limit}
\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}\otimes(J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n})
             -u\otimes u}_{L^{3/2}}
 \le 
\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}}_{L^3}
\norm{J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}
       +
\norm{u}_{L^3}
\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}
 \longrightarrow0\,.
\end{equation}
This gives (S3) for every vector test, with the pressure sign in
Definition~\ref{def:sws}.

Take now a nonnegative $\psi\in C_c^\infty(\R^3\times(0,T))$. In the
right side of the identity of Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-local-energy}, strong $L^3$ convergence gives
$\abs{u_{\varepsilon_n}}^2\to\abs{u}^2$ in $L^{3/2}$, since
\begin{equation*}
 \bigl\|\abs{u_{\varepsilon_n}}^2-\abs{u}^2\bigr\|_{L^{3/2}}
 \le \bigl(
\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}}_{L^3}+
\norm{u}_{L^3}\bigr)
\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}\longrightarrow0\,.
\end{equation*}
The additional mollified transport factor in Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-local-energy} causes no loss:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leray-energy-flux-limit}
\begin{aligned}
 \bigl\|\abs{u_{\varepsilon_n}}^2J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n}
                 -\abs{u}^2u\bigr\|_{L^1}
 &\le 
\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}}_{L^3}^2
\norm{J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}\\
 &\quad+\bigl(
\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}}_{L^3}+
\norm{u}_{L^3}\bigr)
\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}
\norm{u}_{L^3}
 \longrightarrow0\,.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Also,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leray-pressure-velocity-limit}
\norm{p_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n}-pu}_{L^1}
 \le 
\norm{p_{\varepsilon_n}-p}_{L^{3/2}}
\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}}_{L^3}
       +
\norm p_{L^{3/2}}
\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}
 \longrightarrow0\,.
\end{equation}
Multiplication by $\sqrt\psi$ is bounded on $L^2$, so the weak gradient
convergence gives
\begin{equation*}
 2\iint\abs{Du}^2\psi\le\liminf_{n\to\infty}
        2\iint\abs{Du_{\varepsilon_n}}^2\psi\,.
\end{equation*}
Passing to the limit in Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-local-energy} now yields (S4) with $f=0$. These verifications
hold on every finite slab, and the same $u,Du,p$ work for all
$q>5/2$. Thus $(u,Du,p,0)$ is suitable on $\R^3\times(0,\infty)$ for
every such $q$, proving Theorem~\ref{thm:leray}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:ckn-headline}]
Choose any $q>5/2$, for example $q=3$. Theorem~\ref{thm:leray} gives a
suitable weak solution on $\R^3\times(0,\infty)$ in the exact class of
Definition~\ref{def:sws}. Apply the Caffarelli--Kohn--Nirenberg theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:C}, to this solution with $\Omega=\R^3$, $I=(0,\infty)$,
and $f=0$. It follows that the singular set has zero one-dimensional
parabolic Hausdorff measure, as asserted.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Departure from~\cite{ESS2003}]
Theorem~1.1 of~\cite[printed p.~212]{ESS2003} states Leray's existence result but does not
supply the approximation and suitability proof used here. We obtain the
solution from the regularized equations, retain weak limits of every time
slice, and pass the regularized momentum and local energy identities directly
to the limit. In the energy inequality, the dissipation is controlled by weak
lower semicontinuity in $L^2$; the available gradient convergence is weak and
does not justify a Fatou argument.
\end{remark}



\section{Forced Leray solutions}\label{sec:forced-leray}

The force enters the construction in two different ways. Its solenoidal part
does work in the energy equation, while its gradient part is represented by a
pressure. The latter pressure is local in the space-time integrability needed
for suitability, but it need not have the global integrability of the
quadratic pressure. We first fix this pressure split, then add the force to
the regularized construction and pass to the limit.

\subsection{The pressure of the force}

\begin{lemma}[Force pressure]\label{lem:force-pressure}
Let $0<T<\infty$ and $f\in L^2(\R^3\times(0,T);\R^3)$. There is a unique
$p_f\in L^2((0,T);L^6(\R^3))$ obtained from the Fourier multiplier
\begin{equation}\label{eq:force-pressure-multiplier}
 \widehat{p_f}(\xi,t)=-\frac{i\,\xi\cdot\widehat f(\xi,t)}
                              {2\pi|\xi|^2}\quad(\xi\ne0)\,,
\end{equation}
such that, in spatial distributions for almost every $t$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:force-pressure-identities}
 \Delta p_f=\dv f,\qquad \nabla p_f=(I-\Leray)f,\qquad
 \norm{p_f}_{L^2_tL^6_x}\le C\norm f_{L^2_{t,x}}\,.
\end{equation}
Here $C$ depends only on the dimension. On every bounded cylinder
$K\times J\subset\R^3\times(0,T)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:force-pressure-local}
 \norm{p_f}_{L^{3/2}(K\times J)}
 \le |K|^{1/2}|J|^{1/6}\norm{p_f}_{L^2_tL^6_x}\,.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For a smooth force whose spatial Fourier transform is supported in an
annulus, the multiplier in \eqref{eq:force-pressure-multiplier} defines a
function in $H^1(\R^3)$ at each time. The Fourier convention of
Section~\ref{sec:regularised} gives
\[
 \widehat{\nabla p_f}(\xi,t)
   =\frac{\xi\otimes\xi}{|\xi|^2}\widehat f(\xi,t),
 \qquad
 \widehat{\Delta p_f}(\xi,t)=2\pi i\xi\cdot\widehat f(\xi,t).
\]
Plancherel therefore gives $\norm{\nabla p_f(t)}_2\le\norm{f(t)}_2$.
The homogeneous Sobolev inequality used in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-pressure-L2} gives
$\norm{p_f(t)}_6\le C\norm{f(t)}_2$. Approximation of $f$ in
$L^2_{t,x}$ by smooth forces with annular Fourier cutoffs makes the
corresponding $p_f$ Cauchy in $L^2_tL^6_x$. The limit has the stated
multiplier and identities in distributions, and the same bound. The
construction is a bounded linear map from $L^2_{t,x}$ to $L^2_tL^6_x$,
so it also gives a strongly measurable representative.

If two such pressures have the same Laplacian, their difference is harmonic
in space for almost every time and belongs to $L^6(\R^3)$. The mean-value
property on balls of radius $R$ bounds its value at any point by
$C R^{-1/2}$ times its global $L^6$ norm; letting $R\to\infty$ shows that
the difference is zero. Finally, the finite-measure embeddings
$L^6(K)\hookrightarrow L^{3/2}(K)$ and
$L^2(J)\hookrightarrow L^{3/2}(J)$ give
\eqref{eq:force-pressure-local}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:assoc-pressure-forced}]
Let $p_N=P[u\otimes u]$ and let $p_f$ be given by
Lemma~\ref{lem:force-pressure}. The forced energy class gives
$u\in L^\infty_tL^2_x$ and $Du\in L^2_{t,x}$ on $(0,T)$. The interpolation
calculation in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:u-ten-thirds}, before its final
use of the unforced energy bound, gives $u\in L^{10/3}_{t,x}$. Thus
$u\otimes u\in L^{5/3}_{t,x}$ and the boundedness of $P$ gives
$p_N\in L^{5/3}_{t,x}$. Estimate~\eqref{eq:force-pressure-local} now implies
$p=p_N+p_f\in L^{3/2}_{\mathrm{loc}}$ on the cylinder.

Set $r=f-\nabla p_f$. The force-pressure multiplier gives $r=\Leray f\in L^2_{t,x}$ and $\dv r=0$ in space for almost every time. For the solenoidal part of the Helmholtz decomposition, the forced weak identity applied to $w_R$ therefore has source term $-\iint r\cdot w_R$: the gradient part of $f$ pairs to zero with the divergence-free field $w_R$. The cutoff fields in Lemma~\ref{lem:helmholtz-test} also satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:forced-helmholtz-L2}
 \norm{w_R-\varphi^\sigma}_{L^2(\R^3\times J)}\le C_\varphi R^{-3/2}\,,
\end{equation}
where $J$ is the compact time support of $\varphi$. Indeed, $w_R-\varphi^\sigma=\curl((\chi_R-1)A)$; outside $B_R$ the derivative of $A$ is bounded by $C_\varphi(1+|x|)^{-3}$, and the annular term $R^{-1}A$ is bounded by $C_\varphi R^{-3}$. Their squared integrals are $O(R^{-3})$.

For the gradient part, the velocity and $p_N$ terms evaluated on $\nabla(\chi_R\psi)$ cancel by the gradient-test argument in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:assoc-pressure}; the residual work vanishes because $\dv r=0$. The pointwise decay estimates in Lemma~\ref{lem:helmholtz-test} dominate the vector cutoff tests $w_R$ and $\nabla(\chi_R\psi)$ and their time derivatives by a common spatial profile $C_\varphi(1+|x|)^{-3}$; their spatial derivatives paired with $u\otimes u$ and $p_N$ decay faster. This profile belongs to $L^2(\R^3)\cap L^{5/2}(\R^3)$. Since $u,Du,r\in L^2_{t,x}$ and $u\otimes u,p_N\in L^{5/3}_{t,x}$, H\"older's inequality makes the corresponding majorants integrable on the finite time support. Dominated convergence therefore removes the Helmholtz cutoffs in the combined identity and gives the arbitrary-test identity with $p_N$ and residual $r$. Finally, $\nabla p_f\in L^2_{t,x}$ and integration by parts against the compactly supported $\varphi$ give $-\iint r\cdot\varphi=-\iint f\cdot\varphi-\iint p_f\dv\varphi$. The last term is integrable because $p_f\in L^2_tL^6_x$. This is the asserted identity with pressure $p_N+p_f$ and force $f$.

When $f=0$, the multiplier gives $p_f=0$. The conclusion is then exactly
Theorem~\ref{thm:assoc-pressure}, including its conditional
$L^\infty_tL^{3/2}_x$ assertion. No such conditional bound is asserted for
the total pressure when $f\ne0$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Regularization with a rough force}

Write $\mathcal P(\xi)=I-\xi\otimes\xi/|\xi|^2$ for $\xi\ne0$ and
$\mathcal P(0)=I$. This is the Fourier multiplier of $\Leray$ on $L^2$.
For fixed $\varepsilon>0$, the forced mild equation is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:reg-mild-forced}
 u_\varepsilon(t)=S(t)J_\varepsilon a
 -\int_0^tK(t-s)F_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon(s))\,ds
 +\int_0^t S(t-s)\Leray f(s)\,ds,
 \qquad
 (F_\varepsilon(v))_{ij}=(J_\varepsilon v)_i v_j\,.
\end{equation}
The last integral is an $L^2$ Bochner integral. Its norm on a time interval
$I$ of length at most $h$ is bounded by
$h^{1/2}\norm f_{L^2(I;L^2)}$.

\begin{lemma}[Forced regularized solution]\label{lem:regularised-forced}
Let $a\in\J$, $f\in L^2(\R^3\times(0,T);\R^3)$, and $\varepsilon>0$.
There is a unique continuous $L^2$ solution of
\eqref{eq:reg-mild-forced} on $[0,T]$. It is weakly divergence free at
every time, has initial value $J_\varepsilon a$, and for every $t\in[0,T]$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:reg-energy-forced}
 \norm{u_\varepsilon(t)}_2^2
 +2\int_0^t\norm{Du_\varepsilon(s)}_2^2\,ds
 \le \norm{J_\varepsilon a}_2^2
   +2\int_0^t\int_{\R^3} f\cdot u_\varepsilon\,dx\,ds\,.
\end{equation}
For every nonnegative $\psi\in C_c^\infty(\R^3\times(0,T))$, with
\[
 p_\varepsilon=P[F_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon)]+p_f\,,
\]
the following local energy inequality holds:
\begin{align}\label{eq:reg-local-energy-forced}
 2\iint |Du_\varepsilon|^2\psi
 &\le \iint |u_\varepsilon|^2(\partial_t\psi+\Delta\psi)\notag\\
 &\quad+\iint\bigl(|u_\varepsilon|^2J_\varepsilon u_\varepsilon
          +2p_\varepsilon u_\varepsilon\bigr)\cdot\nabla\psi
       +2\iint(f\cdot u_\varepsilon)\psi\,.
\end{align}
For every compactly supported smooth vector test $\varphi$ on
$\R^3\times(0,T)$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:reg-momentum-forced}
 \iint\bigl[-u_\varepsilon\cdot\partial_t\varphi
 -(J_\varepsilon u_\varepsilon)_j(u_\varepsilon)_i\partial_j\varphi_i
 + (Du_\varepsilon)_{ij}\partial_j\varphi_i
 -p_\varepsilon\dv\varphi-f\cdot\varphi\bigr]=0\,.
\end{align}
All constants in the finite-time energy bounds below depend only on
$T$, $\norm a_2$, and $\norm f_{L^2((0,T)\times\R^3)}$, uniformly in
$\varepsilon$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For smooth compactly supported $f$, add the Duhamel term in
\eqref{eq:reg-mild-forced} to the contraction map in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-local-mild}. The nonlinear difference estimate is
unchanged. The force term is continuous in $L^2$ and has norm at most
$h^{1/2}\norm f_{L^2(I;L^2)}$ on an interval $I$ of length $h$. Absolute
continuity of the integral of $|f|^2$ makes this bound uniformly small on
short intervals. The same contraction argument therefore gives local
existence and uniqueness. Since the mollified initial datum and the smooth
force belong to every spatial Sobolev space, the differentiated mild
equation gives the regularity used in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:regularised}. In the $H^k$ energy estimate, the force
pairing is bounded by $\norm{f}_{H^k}^2+\norm{u_\varepsilon}_{H^k}^2$;
Gronwall therefore bounds each $H^k$ norm on every finite time interval,
with a constant allowed to depend on the smooth force and its derivatives.
This prevents a finite-time loss of the regularity needed in the cutoff
energy and local-energy calculations.

The Fourier identities used in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:regularised} give
$\mathcal L F_\varepsilon=\dv F_\varepsilon+\nabla P[F_\varepsilon]$,
and Lemma~\ref{lem:force-pressure} gives
$\Leray f=f-\nabla p_f$. Thus the projected mild equation has the
unprojected form
$\partial_tu_\varepsilon-\Delta u_\varepsilon+
\dv F_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon)+\nabla p_\varepsilon=f$.
Multiply this equation by $2u_\varepsilon$ on
$B_R\times(\delta,t)$, using the spatial cutoff $\chi_R$ from that proof.
The transport cancellation and the pressure divergence are unchanged, and
the added term is $2f\cdot u_\varepsilon$. The quadratic-pressure cutoff errors vanish as
$R\to\infty$ by the $L^2$ estimates used for
\eqref{eq:reg-energy-equality}. The additional force-pressure cutoff term
is bounded, on the annulus $A_R=\{R<|x|<2R\}$, by
\[
 \frac C R\int_\delta^t\int_{A_R}|p_f u_\varepsilon|
 \le C\int_\delta^t
       \norm{p_f(s)}_6\norm{u_\varepsilon(s)}_{L^2(A_R)}\,ds\,.
\]
Here H\"older's inequality contributes $|A_R|^{1/3}=O(R)$, which
cancels $R^{-1}$. On $[\delta,t]$, $p_f$ is bounded in $L^6$ and the
continuous $L^2$ curve $u_\varepsilon(s)$ has uniformly small tails,
so this term tends to zero. The force pairing is integrable by
Cauchy--Schwarz, and the cutoff force integrals converge by dominated
convergence. Letting $\delta\downarrow0$ using continuity in $L^2$
gives the energy equality for this smooth force. In particular,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:reg-energy-forced-gronwall}
 \norm{u_\varepsilon(t)}_2^2
 +2\int_0^t\norm{Du_\varepsilon(s)}_2^2\,ds
 \le \norm a_2^2+\norm{f}_{L^2((0,t)\times\R^3)}^2
       +\int_0^t\norm{u_\varepsilon(s)}_2^2\,ds\,.
\end{equation}
Gronwall bounds the $L^2$ norm on every finite interval, so the local
solution can be restarted before any finite endpoint. This proves global
existence for smooth $f$ and uniqueness follows from the same interval
contraction argument.

For a general $f\in L^2((0,T)\times\R^3)$, choose smooth compactly
supported $f_m\to f$ strongly in $L^2$ and solve the smooth-force equation.
Estimate \eqref{eq:reg-energy-forced-gronwall} bounds these solutions in
$C([0,T];L^2)$ and their gradients in $L^2_{t,x}$, uniformly in $m$.
On each short interval, subtracting the two mild equations and using the
Volterra estimate of Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-local-mild} shows convergence in
$C_tL^2_x$ when $f_m$ is Cauchy in $L^2_{t,x}$. A finite subdivision of
$[0,T]$ gives convergence on the whole interval. The limit is the unique
continuous mild solution; weak compactness of the gradients gives its
gradient data.

The force work converges for every $t$ because
$f_m\to f$ in $L^2_{t,x}$ and $u_{\varepsilon,m}\to u_\varepsilon$ in
$C_tL^2_x$. Weak lower semicontinuity of the gradient norm therefore gives
\eqref{eq:reg-energy-forced} at every time. For fixed $\varepsilon$, the
map $v\mapsto F_\varepsilon(v)$ is continuous from $L^2$ to $L^2$ on
bounded sets: $J_\varepsilon$ maps $L^2$ to $L^\infty$, so strong
$C_tL^2_x$ convergence gives strong $L^2_{t,x}$ convergence of the
tensors. The $L^2$ pressure bound in Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-multiplier-bounds}
then gives strong convergence of their quadratic pressures. The force
pressures converge in $L^2_tL^6_x$ by
Lemma~\ref{lem:force-pressure}. Passing to the limit in the smooth weak
momentum identities proves \eqref{eq:reg-momentum-forced}.

For each smooth-force solution, multiplication by
$2u_{\varepsilon,m}\psi$ gives equality in
\eqref{eq:reg-local-energy-forced}. The velocity-square and transport
terms converge locally by strong $C_tL^2_x$ convergence and the
$L^2\to L^\infty$ bound for $J_\varepsilon$. The force term converges by
Cauchy--Schwarz. For the pressure flux, the quadratic pressure converges
strongly in $L^2_{t,x}$ and the velocity strongly in $L^2$ locally; the
force pressure converges in $L^2_tL^6_x$, while the velocities converge in
$L^2_tL^{6/5}_x$ on the compact spatial support. Finally,
$\sqrt\psi Du_{\varepsilon,m}$ converges weakly in $L^2$, so its squared
norm is lower semicontinuous. These facts give the inequality
\eqref{eq:reg-local-energy-forced}. The construction on successive finite
intervals agrees by mild uniqueness, so it defines one solution on every
finite time interval.
\end{proof}
\subsection{Bounds and spatial tails}

For $T>0$ put
\begin{align*}
 E_T&=e^T\left(\norm a_2^2+
                \norm f_{L^2((0,T)\times\R^3)}^2\right),\\
 G_T^2&=\frac12\left(\norm a_2^2+
                \norm f_{L^2((0,T)\times\R^3)}^2+T E_T\right).
\end{align*}
These quantities are finite and independent of $\varepsilon$.

\begin{lemma}[Forced energy bounds]\label{lem:forced-energy-bounds}
For the solutions of Lemma~\ref{lem:regularised-forced},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:forced-energy-bounds}
 \sup_{0\le t\le T}\norm{u_\varepsilon(t)}_2^2\le E_T,
 \qquad \int_0^T\norm{Du_\varepsilon(t)}_2^2\,dt\le G_T^2\,.
\end{equation}
Consequently,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:forced-ten-thirds}
 \norm{u_\varepsilon}_{L^{10/3}(\R^3\times(0,T))}^{10/3}
 +\norm{J_\varepsilon u_\varepsilon}_{L^{10/3}(\R^3\times(0,T))}^{10/3}
 \le C E_T^{2/3}G_T^2\,,
\end{equation}
and, with $p_{N,\varepsilon}=P[F_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon)]$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:forced-pressure-bounds}
 \norm{p_{N,\varepsilon}}_{L^{5/3}(\R^3\times(0,T))}
 +\norm{p_{N,\varepsilon}}_{L^{3/2}(\R^3\times(0,T))}
 \le C_T\,.
\end{equation}
Here $C_T$ depends only on $T$, $\norm a_2$, $\norm f_{L^2((0,T)\times\R^3)}$, and the dimension-three interpolation and Riesz-transform constants.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Use $2f\cdot u_\varepsilon\le |f|^2+|u_\varepsilon|^2$ in
\eqref{eq:reg-energy-forced}. The resulting inequality and Gronwall give
the first bound in \eqref{eq:forced-energy-bounds}. Substitution back into
the same inequality gives
\[
 2\int_0^T\norm{Du_\varepsilon(t)}_2^2\,dt
 \le \norm a_2^2+\norm f_{L^2((0,T)\times\R^3)}^2+T E_T\,,
\]
which is the second bound.

For almost every $t$, the weak gradient and the homogeneous Sobolev
inequality give the spatial interpolation estimate
\[
 \norm{u_\varepsilon(t)}_{10/3}^{10/3}
 \le C\norm{u_\varepsilon(t)}_2^{4/3}
             \norm{Du_\varepsilon(t)}_2^2\,.
\]
Integrating in time and using Young's convolution inequality for
$J_\varepsilon$ proves \eqref{eq:forced-ten-thirds}. Thus
$F_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon)\in L^{5/3}_{t,x}$, and the $L^{5/3}$
Riesz-transform bound in Definition~\ref{def:riesz-pressure} proves the
first pressure bound. On the finite slab, the same tensor is in $L^1$ with
\[
 \norm{F_\varepsilon(u_\varepsilon)}_{L^1_{t,x}}
 \le T E_T\,.
\]
Interpolation between $L^1$ and $L^{5/3}$ gives the $L^{3/2}$ bound in
\eqref{eq:forced-pressure-bounds}; the constants have the stated
dependence.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Forced spatial tails]\label{lem:forced-tails}
There is a constant $C$ depending only on the dimension, the cutoff profile,
and the Riesz-transform bounds such that for $0<R_1<R_2$,
$0<\varepsilon\le1$, and $0\le t\le T$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:forced-tails}
 \int_{|x|>R_2}|u_\varepsilon(x,t)|^2\,dx
 &\le \int_{|x|>R_1}|J_\varepsilon a(x)|^2\,dx
 +\frac{C\bigl(E_T^{1/2}G_T T^{1/2}
                  +E_T^{3/4}G_T^{3/2}T^{1/4}\bigr)}{R_2-R_1}\notag\\
 &\quad+2E_T^{1/2}T^{1/2}
   \norm{(\Leray f)\,\mathbf 1_{\{|x|>R_1\}}}_{L^2((0,T)\times\R^3)}\,.
\end{align}
Also
\begin{equation}\label{eq:forced-initial-tail}
 \int_{|x|>R_1}|J_\varepsilon a(x)|^2\,dx
 \le \int_{|x|>R_1-1}|a(x)|^2\,dx\,.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Set $D=R_2-R_1$ and choose a smooth radial function $\zeta$ which is zero
on $B_{R_1}$, one outside $B_{R_2}$, and satisfies
$|\nabla\zeta|\le C/D$. First take a smooth force approximation from the
proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:regularised-forced}, and multiply its classical
equation by $2u_\varepsilon\zeta\chi_N$ on $(\delta,t)$, where $\chi_N$
is one on $B_N$, zero outside $B_{2N}$, and
$|\nabla\chi_N|\le C/N$. Spatial integration by parts gives the usual
viscous, transport, and quadratic-pressure fluxes from the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-tails}. The remaining force and force-pressure terms
combine exactly: since $\nabla p_f=(I-\Leray)f$ and the velocity is
divergence free,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:forced-projection-cancellation}
 \int f\cdot u_\varepsilon q+\int p_f u_\varepsilon\cdot\nabla q
   =\int (\Leray f)\cdot u_\varepsilon q
\end{equation}
for each compactly supported scalar cutoff $q$. The identity follows by
integrating $\nabla p_f\cdot(u_\varepsilon q)$ by parts; there is no
boundary term because $q$ is compactly supported.

Let $N\to\infty$ in the cutoff identity. The endpoint terms and the
dissipation converge by dominated convergence. The terms containing
$\nabla\chi_N$ vanish: their absolute values are bounded by $C/N$ times
the space-time integrals of $|u_\varepsilon||Du_\varepsilon|$,
$|J_\varepsilon u_\varepsilon||u_\varepsilon|^2$, and
$|p_{N,\varepsilon}||u_\varepsilon|$. These are finite on $(\delta,t)$.
For the last two, the Gagliardo--Nirenberg estimate in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-tails} gives
\[
 \norm{u_\varepsilon(s)}_4^2
 +\norm{p_{N,\varepsilon}(s)}_2
 \le C E_T^{1/4}\norm{Du_\varepsilon(s)}_2^{3/2}\,;
\]
the force pressure has already been removed by
\eqref{eq:forced-projection-cancellation}. Letting $\delta\downarrow0$
is justified by the energy inequality and the same integrable bounds.

The initial term is at most the first term on the right of
\eqref{eq:forced-tails}. For the cutoff-gradient errors, the viscous term
is bounded by
$C D^{-1}E_T^{1/2}G_TT^{1/2}$. The transport and quadratic-pressure terms
are each bounded by
$C D^{-1}E_T^{3/4}G_T^{3/2}T^{1/4}$, by H\"older's inequality in time
and the preceding Gagliardo--Nirenberg bound. The remaining projected-force
work, which carries the factor $2$ from multiplying the local energy
balance by $2u_\varepsilon$, is bounded by
\[
 2\int_0^t\!\int_{|x|>R_1}|\Leray f|\,|u_\varepsilon|
 \le 2\,T^{1/2}E_T^{1/2}
       \norm{(\Leray f)\mathbf 1_{\{|x|>R_1\}}}_{L^2((0,T)\times\R^3)}\,.
\]
This proves \eqref{eq:forced-tails} for smooth forces. For rough $f$, the
smooth-force solutions converge in $C_tL^2_x$ on finite slabs, their
gradients converge weakly in $L^2_{t,x}$, and $\Leray f_m\to\Leray f$
strongly in $L^2_{t,x}$. Lower semicontinuity and convergence of the other
terms pass the estimate to the solution of Lemma~\ref{lem:regularised-forced}.

Finally, Jensen's inequality and
$\supp\rho_\varepsilon\subset B_\varepsilon$ give
\[
 \int_{|x|>R_1}|J_\varepsilon a(x)|^2\,dx
 \le\int_{|y|>R_1-\varepsilon}|a(y)|^2\,dy
 \le\int_{|y|>R_1-1}|a(y)|^2\,dy\,,
\]
which proves \eqref{eq:forced-initial-tail}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Spatial tail of the projected force]
The force contribution in \eqref{eq:forced-tails} is the tail of $\Leray f$.
The pressure $p_f$ cancels the gradient component of $f$ in the localized
energy identity, so the relevant field is its solenoidal projection. For a
general $L^2$ force this projection is nonlocal; its tail tends to zero
because $\Leray f\in L^2$, but it is not bounded by the tail of $f$ with the
same radius. This is the form of the estimate used below.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Forced time modulus]\label{lem:forced-equicontinuity}
Fix $T>0$ and a compact set $K\subset\R^3$. There is a finite constant
$C_{T,K}$, independent of $0<\varepsilon\le1$, $s,t\in[0,T]$, and
$w\in C_c^\infty(\R^3;\R^3)$ supported in $K$, such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:forced-time-modulus}
 \left|\int (u_\varepsilon(x,t)-u_\varepsilon(x,s))\cdot w(x)\,dx\right|
 \le C_{T,K}\Bigl(\norm{\Delta w}_2+\norm{\nabla w}_{\infty}
   +\norm w_2+\norm{\dv w}_{\infty}\Bigr)|t-s|^{2/5}\,.
\end{equation}
The constant depends only on $T,K,\norm a_2,\norm f_{L^2((0,T)\times\R^3)}$,
and the dimension-three operator bounds.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For $0<s<t$, pair \eqref{eq:reg-momentum-forced} with $w$ between the two
times. The heat and transport terms are bounded by
$E_T^{1/2}\norm{\Delta w}_2(t-s)$ and
$E_T\norm{\nabla w}_{\infty}(t-s)$. The quadratic pressure term is bounded
by $C_T|K|^{2/5}\norm{\dv w}_{\infty}(t-s)^{2/5}$ using
$p_{N,\varepsilon}\in L^{5/3}_{t,x}$. The force term satisfies
\[
 \left|\int_s^t\!\int f\cdot w\right|
 \le |t-s|^{1/2}\norm f_{L^2((0,T)\times\R^3)}\norm w_2\,.
\]
By H\"older's inequality in space and Lemma~\ref{lem:force-pressure},
the force-pressure term is bounded by
\[
 |t-s|^{1/2}\norm{p_f}_{L^2((0,T);L^6)}
     \norm{\dv w}_{L^{6/5}(K)}
 \le |t-s|^{1/2}|K|^{5/6}\norm{p_f}_{L^2_tL^6_x}
     \norm{\dv w}_{\infty}\,.
\]
When $|t-s|\le1$, both $|t-s|$ and $|t-s|^{1/2}$ are at most
$|t-s|^{2/5}$. When $|t-s|\ge1$, the endpoint bound
$2E_T^{1/2}\norm w_2$ suffices. Continuity in $L^2$ handles $s=0$.
\end{proof}


\subsection{Compactness and the forced limit}

\begin{proposition}[Compactness with a fixed force]\label{prop:forced-limit}
Let $a\in\J$, let $f\in L^2((0,T)\times\R^3)$ for every finite $T$, and
let $\varepsilon_n\downarrow0$. There are a subsequence and measurable
fields $u\colon\R^3\times[0,\infty)\to\R^3$ and
$Du\colon\R^3\times(0,\infty)\to\R^{3\times3}$ such that for every
finite $T$,
\begin{align}
 u_{\varepsilon_n}&\longrightarrow u
       &&\text{strongly in }L^2(\R^3\times(0,T)),\label{eq:forced-limit-L2}\\
 Du_{\varepsilon_n}&\rightharpoonup Du
       &&\text{weakly in }L^2(\R^3\times(0,T)),\label{eq:forced-limit-Du}\\
 u_{\varepsilon_n}&\longrightarrow u
       &&\text{strongly in }L^r(\R^3\times(0,T))\quad(2\le r<10/3),
       \label{eq:forced-limit-Lr}\\
 J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n}&\longrightarrow u
       &&\text{strongly in }L^3(\R^3\times(0,T)).\label{eq:forced-limit-JL3}
\end{align}
For every $t>0$, $u_{\varepsilon_n}(\cdot,t)\rightharpoonup u(\cdot,t)$
in $L^2(\R^3)$, and $Du(\cdot,t)$ is the weak spatial gradient of
$u(\cdot,t)$ for almost every $t>0$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Fix a finite $T$. Lemma~\ref{lem:forced-energy-bounds} supplies the uniform
slice and gradient bounds in Lemma~\ref{lem:compactness}. When its test
field $w$ is replaced by a translate of a fixed spatial mollifier, the
proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:forced-equicontinuity} gives a time modulus
independent of the translate: all spatial norms of $w$ are translation
invariant, and on the fixed compact set of centers the $L^{6/5}$ and
$L^{5/2}$ norms of $\dv w$ are bounded by its supremum norm times a
constant depending only on the set. The extra force terms carry the
factor $\norm w_2|t-s|^{1/2}$ and obey the same uniform bound. The
Arzel\`a--Ascoli and spatial-mollification proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:compactness} therefore gives local strong $L^2$ convergence,
weak convergence of every time slice, and local weak convergence of the
gradients. A diagonal extraction over integer time intervals, followed by
weak extraction in global $L^2$ for the gradients, gives one subsequence
for all finite $T$.

We upgrade the velocity convergence using the per-solution exterior estimate in Lemma~\ref{lem:forced-tails}, whose constants are uniform for $0<\varepsilon\le1$ and $0\le t\le T$.
Given $\eta>0$, first take $R_1>1$ so that the $L^2$ tail of $a$ outside
$B_{R_1-1}$ and the $L^2$ tail of $\Leray f$ outside $B_{R_1}$ on
$(0,T)$ are each small enough that their contributions in
\eqref{eq:forced-tails} sum to less than $\eta/2$. Such a choice is
possible because both fields belong to $L^2$. Next choose $R_2>R_1$ so
that the term with denominator $R_2-R_1$ is less than $\eta/2$. Then
\[
 \sup_n\sup_{0\le t\le T}\int_{|x|>R_2}|u_{\varepsilon_n}(x,t)|^2\,dx
 \le\eta\,.
\]
Weak convergence on every time slice and lower semicontinuity on bounded
annuli give the same tail bound for $u(\cdot,t)$, first on
$R_2<|x|<S$ and then on $|x|>R_2$ by monotone convergence. Thus the
space-time $L^2$ tails of the approximants and the limit are uniformly
small. Local strong convergence then gives strong convergence on
$\R^3\times(\delta,T)$ for every $\delta>0$. On the initial strip the
energy bound gives
\[
 \int_0^\delta\int_{\R^3}|u_{\varepsilon_n}|^2\,dx\,dt
 \le \delta E_T\,;
\]
a subsequence converging almost everywhere and Fatou's lemma give the same
bound for $u$. Sending $\delta\downarrow0$ proves
\eqref{eq:forced-limit-L2}.

The $L^{10/3}$ bounds in Lemma~\ref{lem:forced-energy-bounds} and Fatou's
lemma give a uniform $L^{10/3}$ bound for the limit. Interpolation with
\eqref{eq:forced-limit-L2} proves \eqref{eq:forced-limit-Lr}. In
particular the convergence holds in $L^3$. Spatial convolution is
contractive on $L^3$, and $J_{\varepsilon_n}u\to u$ strongly in $L^3$
by the approximate-identity theorem. Hence
\[
 \norm{J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}
 \le\norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}
     +\norm{J_{\varepsilon_n}u-u}_{L^3}\longrightarrow0\,.
\]
Finally, local weak slice convergence and the uniform tails extend the slice convergence from each ball to every global $L^2$ test, by density. This also identifies the representative obtained from iterated mollified limits. If $\sigma$ denotes the compactness reindexing, then for every point, component, and fixed mollifier scale the corresponding pairing converges along $\sigma$ by the all-time weak slice convergence just proved. Restricting that convergent sequence once more along $\sigma$ leaves its limit unchanged, so the limit along $\sigma\circ\sigma$ agrees with the one along $\sigma$. Thus the iterated mollified-limit representative is the same $u$. This proves the proposition.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:leray-forced}]
Choose $\varepsilon_n\downarrow0$ and take the fields from
Proposition~\ref{prop:forced-limit}. Set $p_N=P[u\otimes u]$ and let
$p_f$ be the force pressure from Lemma~\ref{lem:force-pressure}; put
$p=p_N+p_f$. We check the global Leray--Hopf clauses and local suitability.

The energy and gradient bounds in (LH1) follow from
Lemma~\ref{lem:forced-energy-bounds} and weak lower semicontinuity. The
slice weak-gradient property follows from Proposition~\ref{prop:forced-limit}.
Weak divergence freedom at each positive time follows by passing to the
limit in the corresponding identity for $u_{\varepsilon_n}(t)$ on compact
spatial tests. The same proposition gives weak convergence of each slice.

For $w\in C_c^\infty(\R^3;\R^3)$, the time-pairing estimate in the proof
of Lemma~\ref{lem:forced-equicontinuity} is uniform in $\varepsilon$ and
tends to zero with $|t-s|$. Passing to the limit gives continuity of
$t\mapsto\int u(t)\cdot w$ on $(0,T)$. At zero, use
$u_{\varepsilon_n}(0)=J_{\varepsilon_n}a$ and
$J_{\varepsilon_n}a\to a$ in $L^2$; the same uniform modulus gives
$u(t)\rightharpoonup a$ as $t\downarrow0$. The uniform energy bound and
density of compactly supported smooth tests extend weak continuity to
every $L^2$ test and across both endpoints of $[0,T]$. This proves (LH2).

For an arbitrary compactly supported vector test $\varphi$, pass to the
limit in \eqref{eq:reg-momentum-forced}. The time and viscous terms pass by
strong $L^2$ convergence of $u_{\varepsilon_n}$ and weak $L^2$
convergence of $Du_{\varepsilon_n}$. The force term is fixed and pairs
continuously with the strong $L^2$ limit. For the transport term,
\[
 \norm{(J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n})\otimes
            u_{\varepsilon_n}-u\otimes u}_{L^{3/2}}
 \le \norm{J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n}}_{L^3}
          \norm{u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}
      +\norm{J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n}-u}_{L^3}
          \norm u_{L^3}\longrightarrow0\,.
\]
The same product estimate and boundedness of $P$ give
$p_{N,\varepsilon_n}\to p_N$ strongly in $L^{3/2}$ on each finite slab;
$p_f$ is fixed. Thus the pressure term also converges, and the limit is
(S3) with force $f$. Restricting to solenoidal tests gives (FLH1).

Fix $t_0\in(0,T]$. The energy inequality
\eqref{eq:reg-energy-forced} holds at that time for every $n$. The
left-hand side is lower semicontinuous under the weak slice convergence
and weak gradient convergence on $(0,t_0)$. On the right,
\[
 \int_0^{t_0}\!\int f\cdot u_{\varepsilon_n}
   \longrightarrow \int_0^{t_0}\!\int f\cdot u
\]
by $f\in L^2_{t,x}$ and strong convergence in
\eqref{eq:forced-limit-L2}; also $J_{\varepsilon_n}a\to a$ in $L^2$.
Thus the every-time forced energy inequality holds. At $t_0=0$ it is an
identity. The work term tends to zero as $t_0\downarrow0$, since
\[
 \left|\int_0^{t_0}\!\int f\cdot u\right|
 \le \norm f_{L^2((0,t_0)\times\R^3)}
       t_0^{1/2}\sup_{0\le t\le T}\norm{u(t)}_2\longrightarrow0\,.
\]
Weak convergence to $a$ at zero and the energy inequality now give
$\limsup_{t\downarrow0}\norm{u(t)}_2\le\norm a_2$; the
Hilbert-space identity for $\norm{u(t)-a}_2^2$ proves strong attainment
(LH5).

We now pass the local energy inequality. The quadratic pressures
$p_{N,\varepsilon_n}=P[(J_{\varepsilon_n}u_{\varepsilon_n})\otimes
u_{\varepsilon_n}]$ converge strongly to $p_N$ in $L^{3/2}$ by
\eqref{eq:forced-limit-JL3} and boundedness of $P$ on $L^{3/2}$. The force
pressure $p_f$ is fixed. On any compact cylinder it belongs to $L^2$, so
its product with $u_{\varepsilon_n}$ converges in $L^1$ by strong local
$L^2$ convergence. The cubic transport flux converges in $L^1$ by the
strong $L^3$ convergences in Proposition~\ref{prop:forced-limit}; the
force work converges by $f\in L^2_{\mathrm{loc}}$ and the same strong
$L^2$ convergence. Finally, multiplication by $\sqrt\psi$ is bounded on
$L^2$, so weak convergence of the gradients gives lower semicontinuity of
$\int|Du|^2\psi$. Passing to the limit in
\eqref{eq:reg-local-energy-forced} gives (S4), with precisely the term
$2(f\cdot u)\psi$.

The remaining local clauses follow from the energy bounds, the pressure
split, and the assumed local $L^q$ integrability of $f$. The same fields
$u,Du,p$ were selected without reference to $q$. Thus for every
$q>5/2$ for which $f\in L^q_{\mathrm{loc}}$, the quadruple
$(u,Du,p,f)$ is suitable. This proves Theorem~\ref{thm:leray-forced}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:leray-forced-singularSet}]
Choose any $q>5/2$ for which the force is locally in $L^q$. By
Theorem~\ref{thm:leray-forced}, its witness is suitable in the exact class
of Definition~\ref{def:sws}. Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:C}
on $\R^3\times(0,\infty)$. It gives zero
one-dimensional parabolic Hausdorff measure for the singular set.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Zero-force recovery.}
If $f=0$, the forced weak equation and energy inequality in
Definition~\ref{def:forced-leray-hopf} reduce to (LH3) and (LH4), while its
other clauses are exactly (LH1), (LH2), and (LH5). Hence, for $f=0$, the forced
class and the Leray--Hopf class of Definition~\ref{def:leray-hopf} agree with
the same representative. The
zero-force case of Theorem~\ref{thm:leray-forced} therefore gives
Theorem~\ref{thm:leray} with the same $u,Du,p$; applying Theorem~\ref{thm:C} with any
$q>5/2$ gives Corollary~\ref{cor:ckn-headline} and the zero-force
singular-set conclusion. In Theorem~\ref{thm:assoc-pressure-forced}, the
multiplier gives $p_f=0$, so the pressure is exactly
$P[u\otimes u]$. Its global $L^{5/3}$ bound and, under the critical
$L^\infty_tL^3_x$ hypothesis, the conditional
$L^\infty_tL^{3/2}_x$ bound are those already proved in
Theorem~\ref{thm:assoc-pressure} and Lemma~\ref{lem:assoc-pressure-L3}.
Thus the forced pressure statement specializes to
Theorem~\ref{thm:assoc-pressure}.

\begin{remark}[Departure from~\cite{ESS2003}]
Theorem~1.1 of~\cite[printed p.~212]{ESS2003} is unforced. Here the force
adds work to the every-time energy inequality and a local term to the
suitability inequality; its gradient component also creates the pressure
$p_f$. The force class here does not give a global $L^{5/3}$ bound for this
pressure, so the forced theorem records $p_f$ in $L^2_tL^6_x$ and claims
only local $L^{3/2}$ integrability for the total pressure.

Tsai's suitable-existence theorem uses
$L^2(0,T;(D^{1,2}_0(\Omega))')\cap L^{5/3}_tL^{15/14}_x$ and proves a
global $L^{5/3}$ pressure bound; see Theorem~3.9 and displays
(3.38)--(3.42), pp.~63--66 of~\cite{Tsai2018}. The force class here gives global
$L^2_{t,x}$ on finite slabs and local $L^q$ for some $q>5/2$. The
order-one force pressure is therefore controlled here in $L^2_tL^6_x$,
which suffices locally for suitability but does not give global
$L^{5/3}$ integrability. The unforced regularization, uniform-bound, and
compactness arguments are those of
Lemmas~\ref{lem:reg-local-mild}--\ref{lem:compactness} and
Propositions~\ref{prop:leray-limit}--\ref{prop:leray-pressure-limit};
the new terms are the force work, the projected-force tail, and the fixed
force contribution in the local energy inequality.
\end{remark}

%======================================================================

\appendix

\section{External inputs}\label{sec:external}

The analytic inputs and comparison results are collected below with their
precise scope. Several inputs are proved internally in the formal development;
their designation here does not mean that they are additional axioms.
The Campanato comparison and Aubin--Lions compactness entries are not used
by the direct A/B/C proof route. Constants named in this
appendix are absolute unless a dependence is displayed.

\begin{externalinput}[Calder\'on--Zygmund bounds for the Riesz transforms]\label{ext:CZ}
For $i\in\{1,2,3\}$ let $R_{i}$ denote the $i$-th Riesz transform on $\R^{3}$,
that is, for the Fourier convention with kernel $e^{-\mathrm{i}x\cdot\xi}$,
the multiplier operator with symbol $-\mathrm{i}\xi_{i}/\abs{\xi}$;
equivalently $R_{i}=-\partial_{i}(-\Delta)^{-1/2}$, so that
$R_{i}R_{j}=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(-\Delta)^{-1}$. For every $s\in(1,\infty)$
there is a constant $C_{11}=C_{11}(s)$ such that
\[
  \norm{R_{i}R_{j}g}_{L^{s}(\R^{3})}\le C_{11}(s)\norm{g}_{L^{s}(\R^{3})}
  \qquad\text{for all }g\in L^{s}(\R^{3})\text{ and all }i,j .
\]
For the pressure itself we write $C_{11}=C_{11}(3/2)$; the weak-gradient
route also uses the fixed exponent $6/5$. These fixed-exponent constants
are absolute. The operators are the bounded $L^s$ extensions of the
second Riesz transforms from a dense class, not absolutely convergent
global integrals of the Hessian kernel. Literal kernel formulas are used
only off the source support, or with their proper singular-integral
interpretation. Interpolation is first performed on $L^2\cap L^s$ and
then passed to the completed $L^s$ operator.
Reference: \cite[Ch.~II--III]{Stein1970}.
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Hardy--Littlewood--Sobolev for the Riesz potential of order $1$]
\label{ext:riesz}
Let $N(y)=-\tfrac1{4\pi\abs{y}}$, so that $\abs{\nabla N(y)}=\tfrac1{4\pi\abs{y}^{2}}$.
There is an absolute finite constant $C'_{11}$ such that, for every
$g\in L^{5/2}(\R^3)$ and every $j\in\{1,2,3\}$, the literal convolution
$\partial_jN*g$ is absolutely convergent for almost every $x$ and
\[
  \norm{\partial_jN*g}_{L^{15}(\R^3)}
      \le C'_{11}\norm{g}_{L^{5/2}(\R^3)} .
\]
This is the specialization $1/15=1/(5/2)-1/3$ needed here; the full
Hardy--Littlewood--Sobolev exponent family is not part of this input.
No pointwise measurability or compact-support assumption is added to the
$L^{5/2}$ datum.
Reference: \cite[Ch.~V, Thm.~1]{Stein1970}.
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Newtonian representation of a compactly supported solution]
\label{ext:newtonian}
Let $N(y)=-\tfrac1{4\pi\abs{y}}$, so that $\Delta N=\delta_{0}$ in
$\mathcal{D}'(\R^{3})$. Let $v\in L^{3/2}(\R^{3})$ have compact support and
suppose that in $\mathcal{D}'(\R^{3})$
\[
  \Delta v=\partial_{i}\partial_{j}G_{ij}+\partial_{j}H_{j}+K
\]
where $G\in L^{3/2}(\R^{3};\R^{3\times3})$, $H\in L^{m}(\R^{3};\R^{3})$ for some
$m\in(1,3)$, and $K\in L^{m'}(\R^{3})$ for some $m'\in(1,3/2)$, all with compact
support. Then
\[
  v=-R_{i}R_{j}G_{ij}+\partial_{j}N*H_{j}+N*K
  \qquad\text{almost everywhere in }\R^{3}.
\]
Reference: \cite[Ch.~2 and Ch.~4]{GilbargTrudinger2001} for the Newtonian
potential and \cite[Ch.~III, V]{Stein1970} for the identification
$\partial_{i}\partial_{j}N*G_{ij}=-R_{i}R_{j}G_{ij}$; the uniqueness statement
is Liouville's theorem applied to the difference, which is harmonic on $\R^{3}$
and tends to $0$ in the $L^{3/2}$-average sense at infinity.
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Sobolev embeddings]\label{ext:sobolev-ball}\leavevmode
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] There is an absolute constant such that
  $\norm{g}_{L^{6}(\R^{3})}\le C\norm{\nabla g}_{L^{2}(\R^{3})}$ for all
  $g\in H^{1}(\R^{3})$; consequently
  $\norm{g}_{L^{6}(\R^{3})}\le C\norm{g}_{H^{1}(\R^{3})}$.
\item[(ii)] There is an absolute constant $C$ such that for every Euclidean
  ball $B_r(x)$, $r>0$, and every $g\in H^1(B_r(x))$,
  \[
    \norm{g}_{L^6(B_r(x))}
      \le C\bigl(\norm{\nabla g}_{L^2(B_r(x))}
                       +r^{-1}\norm{g}_{L^2(B_r(x))}\bigr).
  \]
  In particular the unscaled $H^1$ estimate has an absolute constant on $B_1$.
  No general Lipschitz-domain extension theorem is required here.
\item[(iii)] There is an absolute constant with
  $\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}(\R^{3})}\le C\norm{g}_{H^{2}(\R^{3})}$ for all
  $g\in H^{2}(\R^{3})$.
\item[(iv)] Let $J$ be an interval and $U=B_r(x)$, $r>0$. If
  $g\in L^\infty(J;L^2(U))\cap L^2(J;H^1(U))$, then
  $g\in L^{10/3}(U\times J)$ and, with one absolute constant $C$,
  \[
  \norm{g}_{L^{10/3}(U\times J)}^{10/3}
   \le C\bigl(\norm{g}_{L^\infty(J;L^2(U))}^{4/3}
                  \norm{\nabla g}_{L^2(U\times J)}^2
       +r^{-2}\norm{g}_{L^\infty(J;L^2(U))}^{10/3}|J|\bigr).
  \]
  For unbounded $J$, the membership conclusion is asserted separately;
  an infinite right side is not a proof of membership. The displayed
  bound is \eqref{eq:interp-q103} integrated in time with the slice
  $L^2$ mass bounded by the essential supremum. No embedding on an
  arbitrary bounded open set, or sharper public actual-mass estimate,
  is asserted here.
\end{enumerate}
References: \cite[Ch.~7]{GilbargTrudinger2001}, \cite[Ch.~V]{Stein1970}.
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Poincar\'e inequalities on the unit ball]\label{ext:poincare}\leavevmode
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] There is an absolute constant with
  $\norm{g-\spavg{g}{B_{1}}}_{L^{1}(B_{1})}\le C\norm{\nabla g}_{L^{1}(B_{1})}$
  for all $g\in W^{1,1}(B_{1})$.
\item[(ii)] There is an absolute constant with
  $\norm{g-\spavg{g}{B_{1}}}_{L^{6}(B_{1})}\le C\norm{\nabla g}_{L^{2}(B_{1})}$
  for all $g\in H^{1}(B_{1})$.
\item[(iii)] \emph{Additional classical statement.} There is an absolute constant with
  $\norm{g-\spavg g{B_1}}_{L^{3/2}(B_1)}
          \le C\norm{\nabla g}_{L^1(B_1)}$
  for all $g\in W^{1,1}(B_1)$. Rescaling gives the same coefficient on
  any Euclidean ball, since both sides have scaling degree $2$.
  This arbitrary $W^{1,1}$ version is recorded as classical paper material,
  not as an available formal export; the formal scalar estimate presently
  treats $C^1$ data.
\end{enumerate}
Reference: \cite[Ch.~7]{GilbargTrudinger2001}.
\end{externalinput}

The formalized Caccioppoli application uses a separate squared-velocity
estimate: for $0<r<R$, $B_R(x_0)\subset U$ with $U$ open, and
$u\in H^1(U;\R^3)$ with its weak gradient,
\[
 \bigl\||u|^2-\spavg{|u|^2}{B_r(x_0)}\bigr\|_{L^{3/2}(B_r(x_0))}
 \le C\norm u_{L^2(B_r(x_0))}\norm{\nabla u}_{L^2(B_r(x_0))}.
\]
The constant is absolute. Approximation on nested balls proves this
particular weak-slice estimate; it does not by itself export clause (iii)
for arbitrary $W^{1,1}$ functions.

\begin{externalinput}[Interior estimates for harmonic functions]\label{ext:harmonic}
Let $h$ be harmonic on an open $U\subset\R^{3}$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] \emph{(Mean value property.)} If $\overline{\Ball{s}{x}}\subset U$
  then $h(x)=\abs{\Ball{s}{x}}^{-1}\int_{\Ball{s}{x}}h$.
\item[(ii)] \emph{(Gradient estimate.)} If $\overline{\Ball{s}{x}}\subset U$
  then $\abs{\nabla h(x)}\le\tfrac3s\sup_{\Ball{s}{x}}\abs{h}$.
\end{enumerate}
Reference: \cite[Ch.~2]{GilbargTrudinger2001}.
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Vitali covering lemma, $5r$ form]\label{ext:vitali}
Let $(X,d)$ be a \emph{separable} metric space and let $\mathcal{F}$ be a family
of open balls $\mathfrak{B}_{r_{a}}(z_{a})$, $a\in\mathcal{I}$, with
$\sup_{a}r_{a}<\infty$. Then there is a countable set
$\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{I}$ such that the balls
$\{\mathfrak{B}_{r_{j}}(z_{j})\}_{j\in\mathcal{J}}$ are pairwise disjoint and
\[
  \bigcup_{a\in\mathcal{I}}\mathfrak{B}_{r_{a}}(z_{a})
  \subset\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathfrak{B}_{5r_{j}}(z_{j}) .
\]
In particular every $\mathfrak{B}_{r_{a}}(z_{a})$ is contained in some
$\mathfrak{B}_{5r_{j}}(z_{j})$. Separability is needed for the subfamily to be
\emph{countable}: without it the standard Zorn's-lemma argument produces only a
maximal disjoint subfamily, which may be uncountable.
The open-ball version follows directly from the usual $5r$ argument. Choose
$R>\sup_a r_a$ and successively choose a maximal disjoint family among
balls of radii in $(2^{-n-1}R,2^{-n}R]$ that avoid the earlier choices.
Every unchosen ball meets a chosen ball of radius at least half its own,
and is therefore contained in that ball's fivefold enlargement. The chosen
balls are countable because each contains a distinct point of a fixed
countable dense set. This also proves the individual containment assertion.
For the covering argument in its standard form see \cite[Thm.~2.1]{Mattila1995}.
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Hausdorff measures of a metric space]\label{ext:hausdorff}
Let $(X,d)$ be a metric space and $\alpha\ge0$. The set functions
$\Phaus^{\alpha}_{\delta}$ and $\Phaus^{\alpha}$ of
Definition~\ref{def:parabolic-hausdorff} are outer measures on $X$; in
particular $\Phaus^{\alpha}$ is countably subadditive and monotone, and
$\Phaus^{\alpha}(E)=\lim_{\delta\to0^{+}}\Phaus^{\alpha}_{\delta}(E)
 =\sup_{\delta>0}\Phaus^{\alpha}_{\delta}(E)$, the family
$\delta\mapsto\Phaus^{\alpha}_{\delta}(E)$ being nonincreasing in $\delta$.
Reference: \cite[Ch.~4]{Mattila1995}.
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Parabolic Campanato characterization of H\"older continuity]
\label{ext:campanato}
Let $\alpha\in(0,1)$, $R>0$, $z_{0}\in\R^{3}\times\R$, and
$w\in L^{3}(\Cyl{2R}{z_{0}};\R^{m})$. Suppose
\[
  \Lambda^{3}\;:=\;\sup\Bigl\{\frac1{r^{5+3\alpha}}
    \iint_{\Cyl{r}{z}}\bigl|w-\stavg{w}{\Cyl{r}{z}}\bigr|^{3}dz'
    \ :\ z\in \Cyl{R}{z_{0}},\ 0<r\le R\Bigr\}\;<\;\infty .
\]
Then $w$ agrees almost everywhere on $\Cyl{R}{z_{0}}$ with a function
$\bar w\in C^{\alpha,\alpha/2}(\overline{\Cyl{R}{z_{0}}};\R^{m})$, and there is a
constant $C(\alpha)$ with
\[
  [\bar w]_{C^{\alpha,\alpha/2}(\Cyl{R}{z_{0}})}\le C(\alpha)\Lambda,
  \qquad
  \norm{\bar w}_{L^{\infty}(\Cyl{R}{z_{0}})}
   \le C(\alpha)\Bigl(\Lambda R^{\alpha}
     +R^{-5/3}\norm{w}_{L^{3}(\Cyl{2R}{z_{0}})}\Bigr).
\]
The mean $\stavg{w}{\Cyl{r}{z}}$ is the genuine space-time average
\eqref{eq:spacetime-average}. Note that the normalization is
$r^{-5-3\alpha}$, so that in terms of the excess \eqref{eq:excess} the
hypothesis reads $\widetilde C(z,r)^{1/3}\le\Lambda\,r^{1+\alpha}$.
Reference: \cite[\S4.2]{LadyzhenskayaSolonnikovUraltseva1968},
\cite[\S III.1]{Giaquinta1983} for the elliptic model case, and
\cite[after (6.24)]{Tsai2018} for the form used here. This input is recorded for
comparison with the blow-up route only: Section~\ref{sec:core} uses instead the
self-contained Lemma~\ref{lem:campanato}, and the exponent it delivers is
$\gamma_{0}=\min\{2-5/q,1/5\}$ (Remark~\ref{rem:exponent}).
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Heat kernel]\label{ext:heat}
Let $G(x,\tau)=(4\pi\tau)^{-3/2}\exp\bigl(-\abs{x}^{2}/(4\tau)\bigr)$ for
$x\in\R^{3}$, $\tau>0$. Then $G\in C^{\infty}(\R^{3}\times(0,\infty))$, $G>0$,
$\int_{\R^{3}}G(x,\tau)\,dx=1$ for every $\tau>0$, and
$\partial_{\tau}G=\Delta G$ on $\R^{3}\times(0,\infty)$. Consequently, for
$r>0$, the function $\psi_{r}(x,t)=r^{2}G(x,r^{2}-t)$ is smooth and positive on
$\R^{3}\times(-\infty,r^{2})$ and satisfies
$\partial_{t}\psi_{r}+\Delta\psi_{r}=0$ there.
Reference: \cite[Ch.~2]{Tsai2018}.
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Heat kernel: size and derivative bounds]\label{ext:heat-kernel}
Let
\[
W_{+}(x,t)=\mathbf{1}_{t>0}(4\pi t)^{-3/2}e^{-\abs{x}^{2}/(4t)}
.
\] Then
$W_{+}\in L^{1}_{loc}(\R^{3}\times\R)$, $\supp W_{+}\subset\{t\ge0\}$,
$(\partial_{t}-\Delta)W_{+}=\delta_{(0,0)}$ in $\mathcal{D}'(\R^{3}\times\R)$,
and convolution with a compactly supported distribution is well-defined.
Causal uniqueness, if used, must be restricted to tempered distributions:
a tempered $d$ supported in $\{t\ge c\}$ and satisfying
$(\partial_t-\Delta)d=0$ is zero. No such assertion holds for arbitrary
distributions, and half-space support does not define a universal
convolution algebra. The proof below uses the adjoint-test identity
in Lemma~\ref{lem:local-equation}, not this uniqueness assertion.
There is an absolute constant $C$
such that, with
$\varrho=\max\{|x|,|t|^{1/2}\}$, for $t>0$,
\[
  \abs{W_{+}}\le C\varrho^{-3},\quad
  \abs{\nabla_{x}W_{+}}\le C\varrho^{-4},\quad
  \abs{\partial_{t}W_{+}}\le C\varrho^{-5},
\]
and, for every Fourier multiplier $\varsigma(D)$ with $\varsigma$ smooth on
$\R^{3}\setminus\{0\}$ and homogeneous of degree $1$,
\[
  \abs{\varsigma(D)W_{+}}\le C_{\varsigma}\varrho^{-4},\quad
  \abs{\nabla_{x}\varsigma(D)W_{+}}\le C_{\varsigma}\varrho^{-5},\quad
  \abs{\partial_{t}\varsigma(D)W_{+}}\le C_{\varsigma}\varrho^{-6}.
\]
The causal kernels vanish for $t<0$. Their size estimates hold almost
everywhere on spacetime, but no time derivative at $t=0$ is asserted for
the general multiplier kernels.
Reference: \cite[\S IV.11]{LadyzhenskayaSolonnikovUraltseva1968},
\cite[Ch.~5]{LemarieRieusset2016}.
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Aubin--Lions compactness]\label{ext:aubin-lions}
Let $X\subset\subset Y\subset Z$ be Banach spaces with $X$ reflexive, the
embedding $X\hookrightarrow Y$ compact and $Y\hookrightarrow Z$ continuous. Let
$J$ be a bounded interval, $1<s_{0}\le\infty$, $1<s_{1}<\infty$, and let
$(g_{k})$ be a sequence with
$\sup_{k}\bigl(\norm{g_{k}}_{L^{s_{0}}(J;X)}+\norm{\partial_{t}g_{k}}_{L^{s_{1}}(J;Z)}\bigr)<\infty$.
Then $(g_{k})$ is relatively compact in $L^{s_{0}}(J;Y)$ (and in $C(\overline{J};Y)$
if $s_{0}=\infty$ and $s_{1}>1$). For comparison with a compactness proof one may take
$X=H^{1}(B)$, $Y=L^{2}(B)$, $Z=Z_B$ of \eqref{eq:Z-space},
$s_0=2$, $s_1=3/2$. This input is not consumed in the direct proof here.
Reference: \cite[Corollary 4]{Simon1987}, including the time-continuity endpoint.
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Pettis measurability theorem]\label{ext:pettis}
Let $X$ be a separable Banach space, $J\subset\R$ an interval, and
$w:J\to X$ such that $t\mapsto\langle w(t),\varphi\rangle$ is Lebesgue
measurable for every $\varphi\in X^{*}$. Then $w$ is strongly (Bochner)
measurable, i.e.\ an a.e.\ limit of countably valued measurable functions.
In particular, if $X$ is a separable
Hilbert space or the dual of a separable Hilbert space, weak measurability
implies strong measurability.
Reference: \cite[Ch.~II]{DiestelUhl1977}.
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Vector-valued du Bois-Reymond lemma]\label{ext:dubois-reymond}
Let $X$ be a Banach space, $J\subset\R$ an open interval and
$w\in L^{1}_{loc}(J;X)$ with
$\int_{J}w(t)\,\theta'(t)\,dt=0$ in $X$ for every $\theta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(J)$.
Then there is $\xi\in X$ with $w(t)=\xi$ for almost every $t\in J$.
Reference: \cite[Ch.~III, Lemma 1.1]{Temam1977}.
\end{externalinput}

\begin{externalinput}[Mollification and density]\label{ext:mollify}
Let $\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{1})$ with $\zeta\ge0$ and
$\int_{\R^{3}}\zeta=1$, and $\zeta_{\varepsilon}(y)=\varepsilon^{-3}\zeta(y/\varepsilon)$.
For $g\in L^{s}(\R^{3})$, $1\le s<\infty$, one has
$g*\zeta_{\varepsilon}\in C^{\infty}(\R^{3})$,
$\norm{g*\zeta_{\varepsilon}}_{L^{s}}\le\norm{g}_{L^{s}}$ and
$g*\zeta_{\varepsilon}\to g$ in $L^{s}(\R^{3})$; the analogous statements hold in
one time variable. Moreover $C_{c}^{\infty}(U)$ is dense in $H^{2}_{0}(U)$ by
definition of the latter, and $C_{c}^{\infty}(U)$ is dense in $L^{s}(U)$ for
$1\le s<\infty$ and $U\subset\R^{3}$ open.
Reference: \cite[Ch.~7]{GilbargTrudinger2001}.
\end{externalinput}

\section{Corrections to the sources of the existence part}\label{app:errata}

This appendix records where the sources used in this part are corrected or
completed by the arguments above. The corrections to the sources used only in
the endpoint regularity theory are recorded in~\cite{ESSLean}.

\subsection*{Escauriaza--Seregin--\v{S}ver\'ak (2003)}

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Unproved existence citation.} Theorem~1.1, printed p.~212, states Leray's global existence result and cites prior work rather than proving it. The manuscript proves the regularized construction and carries out the suitability and Leray-limit passages in Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-limit}, Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-hopf-limit}, and Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-pressure-limit}, culminating in Theorem~\ref{thm:leray}. This is an addition to the proof, not a correction to the theorem statement.

\item \textbf{Associated pressure normalization and mixed-norm notation.} Equations (3.2)--(3.4), printed p.~221, invoke coercive Stokes estimates and state the pressure equation and \(L_{3/2,\infty}\) bound without an explicit whole-space Riesz formula or pressure normalization. Here \(L_{3/2,\infty}\) means \(L^\infty_tL^{3/2}_x\), not Lorentz \(L^{3/2,\infty}\). Derivative estimates are interior to positive time even though \(\delta_1>0\) is arbitrary. The manuscript fixes a canonical double-Riesz pressure and sign in Definition~\ref{def:riesz-pressure}, proves the arbitrary-test extension in Theorem~\ref{thm:assoc-pressure}, and integrates the \(L^3\)-solution pressure bound in Lemma~\ref{lem:assoc-pressure-L3}.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection*{Ożański--Pooley, arXiv:1708.09787v1}

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Young and convolution hypotheses.} Section~1.1, Lemma~1.1 and the integral Minkowski/Young statements, leaves measurability and integrability conditions implicit; its spatial Young inequality permits positive exponents although the norm inequality needs exponents at least one. The proof also takes a maximum over a closed time interval although the kernel is defined only for positive time. Appendix A.2 repeats this lemma and has a likely time-index typo in its continuity proof. These auxiliary results are not used in the manuscript.

\item \textbf{Mollifier range and weak derivatives.} Section~1.1, Lemma~1.2, states an inhomogeneous \(H^m\) estimate for every \(\varepsilon>0\) with an \(\varepsilon^{-m}\) factor; for large \(\varepsilon\) this needs a restriction such as \(0<\varepsilon\le1\), or a bound with \(1+\varepsilon^{-m}\). The weak-derivative clause also leaves its distributional interpretation implicit. The manuscript uses a fixed compactly supported mollifier with \(0<\varepsilon\le1\) in Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-mollifier-bounds}.

\item \textbf{Undifferentiated Newtonian potential on \(L^2\).} Section~1.1, Definition~1.2, flags that \(L^2\) decay may not make its displayed potential integral pointwise defined. The later differentiated multiplier does not define that undifferentiated integral. The manuscript defines pressure by bounded Riesz extensions in Definition~\ref{def:riesz-pressure}; it does not use that potential formula on arbitrary \(L^2\) data.

\item \textbf{Multiplier scope and value at zero frequency.} Section~1.1, Lemma~1.4, proves only the \(L^2\) multiplier bound, not general \(L^p\) Calderón--Zygmund boundedness, and leaves the multiplier value at \(\xi=0\) unspecified in the Sobolev extension. Lemma~1.3 states its estimate scalarly; a vector version must specify summation over components. The manuscript uses the all-exponent Riesz-transform extension of External Input~\ref{ext:CZ} and records its sign in Definition~\ref{def:riesz-pressure}.

\item \textbf{Oseen kernel display and norms.} Section~1.2, the radial Oseen kernel definition, assigns \(\widetilde P(0)=1\), although the displayed formula's limit is \(1/(4\pi^{3/2}\sqrt t)\). The Oseen tensor estimates also leave the matrix norm implicit, and the proof's split at \(x=y\) is degenerate unless that case is treated separately. This heat/Oseen route is not used in the manuscript's Fourier construction Theorem~\ref{thm:regularised}.

\item \textbf{Pressure variable typo.} Section~2, the pressure representation following Theorem~2.2, writes \(X\) immediately after defining the forcing \(F\); \(X=F\) is intended. The manuscript supplies its own explicit tensor input in Definition~\ref{def:riesz-pressure}.

\item \textbf{Fourier exponential sign typo.} Appendix~A.4.1, in the Fourier proof of Theorem~2.2, displays the transform of \(P\) with \(e^{+4\pi^2t|\xi|^2}\); \(-\Delta P=\Phi\), the next derivative display, and the final Oseen multiplier require a negative exponent. This route is not used in Theorem~\ref{thm:regularised}.

\item \textbf{Nonlinear forcing and weak-form sign/index conflict.} Section~2.2, equations (2.12)--(2.16), declares \(F=-(Y\cdot\nabla)Y\), but its component formula, positive weak nonlinear term, and positive-gradient Oseen representation do not all match that forcing. The displayed pressure sign agrees with the negative divergence forcing. Corollaries~2.4 and 2.7 also use incompatible tensor slots; in Corollary~2.7 the displayed \(F_i=-\partial_i(Y_iZ_k)\) repeats \(i\) and leaves \(k\) free. The same sign inconsistency propagates to Theorem~2.6 and later §3 formulas. The manuscript does not adopt these identities: Theorem~\ref{thm:regularised} derives the weak identity from its pointwise equation, and Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-momentum} fixes the tensor order used here.

\item \textbf{Pressure gauge.} Section~2, Theorem~2.5, establishes uniqueness of velocity, not literal uniqueness of pressure; pressure is determined only modulo a time-only function. Section~3, Corollary~3.3, claims pressure regularity for every pressure allowed by Definition~3.1, which is false after adding an arbitrary locally integrable \(c(t)\). A canonical normalization is required. The manuscript makes this choice in Definition~\ref{def:riesz-pressure}.


\item \textbf{Unbounded-domain energy integrations.} Section~3, Theorem~3.4 and Lemma~3.5, suppress cutoff/density justifications for integration by parts on \(\mathbb R^3\). The source integrability is enough to make a cutoff proof plausible, but the vanishing boundary terms are not shown. These comparison results are not used in the manuscript.

\item \textbf{Mild representation time argument typo.} Section~3, equation (3.5), integrates over absolute \(s\in[t_1,t_2]\) but prints kernel time \(t_2-t_1-s\); the Duhamel time should be \(t_2-s\). The later formula (3.11) uses \(t-s\). This mild representation is not used in Theorem~\ref{thm:regularised}.

\item \textbf{Weak-divergence quantifier and pressure differentiability.} Section~3, Definition~3.1, leaves the time quantifier on weak divergence freedom implicit.
