Corollary~3.3's pressure regularity also omits weak time-differentiation and induction details, in addition to the gauge defect above. These source definitions are not used in the manuscript.

\item \textbf{Strong-solution proof details.} Section~3, Theorem~3.2, sketches the regularity induction without a precise local-time bound; Lemma~3.6 does not claim convergence at \(t=0\) and leaves the nonnegative range of its scalar function implicit; Lemma~3.8's “claim follows” omits separate energy and comparison limit details. These routes are not used in the manuscript.

\item \textbf{Picard existence sign and zero-data defects.} Section~3, Theorem~3.9, combines the standard-sign Oseen iteration with an opposite-sign Stokes equation and weak identity; the displayed proof therefore does not establish its stated Definition~3.7 solution. Its lifespan divides by \(\|u_0\|_\infty^2\), leaving zero data undefined, and the invariant contraction set is not fully stated. The manuscript uses the Fourier--\(L^2\) route in Theorem~\ref{thm:regularised} instead.

\item \textbf{Regularized weak-form mismatch.} Section~4.1, equation (4.2), has a nonlinear sign and tensor-order mismatch with the regularized PDE (4.1) and its later time-sliced identity. For an initial-plus-integral identity with tests supported before \(T\), the sign is opposite as well. The manuscript derives its pressure-inclusive weak identity from the pointwise regularized equation in Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-momentum}.

\item \textbf{Regularized existence typos and zero data.} Section~4.1, Theorem~4.2 proof, uses \(u(s)\) where the argument requires \(u_\varepsilon(s)\), and prints \(\varepsilon^{+3/2}\) where the mollifier estimate and subsequent coefficient require \(\varepsilon^{-3/2}\). Its lifespan \(C/\|u_0\|_\infty^2\) also leaves \(u_0=0\) undefined. The manuscript's Fourier construction Theorem~\ref{thm:regularised} repairs the proof route and uses a lifespan formula defined for zero data.

\item \textbf{Separation-of-energy proof gaps.} Section~4.1, Lemma~4.3, has a missing terminal \(t\) in its localized balance; the following display indicates the intended endpoint. Whole-space cutoff testing and the Newton-potential integration by parts lack truncation and decay arguments, and the theorem's regularity/data context is not restated. Its positive-time regularity hypothesis also requires a limit argument at the initial endpoint. The tail estimate of Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-tails} replaces the Newton-potential step by an \(L^2\) double-Riesz estimate and supplies the cutoff and initial-time limits.

\item \textbf{Global energy-space overclaim.} Section~4.2, Corollary~4.6, claims global \(L^2_tH^1_x\) from the energy inequality. The proof also obtains weak continuity using density of admissible tests in \(L^2\) without spelling out that step. The inequality controls \(u\) in \(L^\infty_tL^2_x\) and \(\nabla u\) in \(L^2_{t,x}\), but does not make \(\int_0^\infty\|u(t)\|_2^2\,dt\) finite. This corollary is not used.

\item \textbf{Global-existence proof display defects.} Section~4.2, Theorem~4.7, has an index typo in its Helly step (“\(k\to\infty\)” instead of the sequence index), and its energy-limit display uses \(\int_0^t\) while equating to the norm at a later start \(s\); the lower-semicontinuity step requires \(\int_s^t\). The simple-function density and time-uniform continuity arguments are also compressed. Definition~4.5 uses an all-time representative, with an a.e.-time gradient, but does not state strong \(L^2\) attainment at zero; Theorem~4.7 proves that trace. Theorem~4.7 itself gives no pressure, suitability, or local energy inequality; its all-space strong convergence uses the tail estimate. The manuscript gives its own compactness and every-time energy passage in Lemma~\ref{lem:compactness}, Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-limit}, and Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-hopf-limit}; it does not rely on Theorem~4.7.

\item \textbf{A.7 gradient sign and decay hypotheses.} Appendix~A.7, printed pp.~75--76, alternative pressure-bound proof, prints \(\nabla_x|x-y|^{-1}=\nabla_y|x-y|^{-1}\); the correct identity has a minus sign, on which the subsequent integration by parts depends. The triple-integral steps also need decay/approximation assumptions on \(g\). This alternative proof is not used.

\item \textbf{Volterra induction exponent typo.} Appendix~A.10, printed pp.~73--75, proof of Theorem~A.10, mixes \(M\| \phi\| C^k\) in the induction hypothesis with \(\|\phi\|M^k\) in the target and \(IC^2M^k\) in the resulting estimate. The written induction is not algebraically aligned; this theorem is not used.

\item \textbf{Approximation exponent mismatch.} Appendix~A.12, printed pp.~76--78, Lemma~A.12, is stated for general \(p\) but its conclusion is in \(L^2\), while its proof estimates \(L^p\). It needs either an \(L^p\) conclusion or restriction to \(p=2\); its cited uses are the latter. This lemma is not used.

\item \textbf{Continuity, trace, and Bochner conventions.} Appendix~A.1--A.5 and A.7 leave some measurability/Bochner hypotheses implicit; the pointwise heat convergence in Lemma~A.1 requires a representative continuous at the point, and “classical” heat/Stokes solutions are only positive-time regular with an \(L^2\) initial trace. Appendix~A.4.3 uses a bound up to time zero although its stated Hölder regularity is only for positive times. Appendix~A.4.4 leaves growth/cutoff hypotheses for mollified pressure and Fubini implicit and writes an \(\mathbb R^n\) test in an \(\mathbb R^3\) argument. Appendix~A.5 and Corollary~A.7 use convolution-continuity and measurability assumptions not fully stated. Appendix~A.5 Corollary~A.6 is justified only by “similar,” with no proof. These auxiliary routes are not used.

\end{enumerate}

\subsection*{Tsai}

\begin{enumerate}
\item Theorem~3.9, printed p.~66, introduces the local energy identity by assertion without deriving the integrations by parts or stating their required regularity/integrability; it also asserts, rather than proves, uniqueness of the harmonic pressure part. This source serves only for comparison; the manuscript proves the identity in Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-local-energy} for its regularized smooth pair.

\item Theorem~3.9 uses Lemma~3.7 for strong local compactness in Step~3, despite the accompanying route description suggesting otherwise; Lemma~3.7 is bounded-domain compactness, and Lemma~3.8 is the bounded-domain pressure branch, not the whole-space branch. The manuscript uses its own compactness result Lemma~\ref{lem:compactness} and carries out the whole-space tail and Leray-limit arguments in Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-tails} and Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-limit}.

\item Lemma~3.4, printed pp.~57--58, first gives an a.e.-time equivalence class; the modified representative is essential for fixed-time assertions. Definition~\ref{def:leray-hopf} retains every-time slices; the manuscript carries out the limit identification in Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-limit}.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection*{Robinson--Rodrigo--Sadowski}

\begin{enumerate}
\item Equation~(14.5), printed pp.~264--265, is labeled formal for weak Navier--Stokes solutions. For the regularized pair, the omitted regularity/integrability and most smooth-pair integrations by parts must be supplied; the source spells out only the nonlinear transport integration.

\item Theorem~14.2, printed pp.~268--272, is a torus suitability proof. Its invocation of whole-space Theorem~B.6 needs the periodic multiplier version, and its pressure convergence in \(L^{3/2}(\mathbb T^3)\) cannot be transplanted as a whole-space pressure argument. Theorem~4.4 and the strong energy theorem~4.6 are likewise stated for periodic or bounded domains, not as whole-space construction results. These are not used; the manuscript provides the whole-space pressure and identity in Definition~\ref{def:riesz-pressure}, Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-local-energy}, and Theorem~\ref{thm:assoc-pressure}.

\item Exercise~14.2 is left as the weak-equation convergence step in Theorem~14.2. Section~14.4, printed pp.~272--273, also needs a nonnegative cutoff test and yields an a.e.-time endpoint after a time approximate identity and subsequence, not an every-time assertion. The manuscript states the space-time local identity in Lemma~\ref{lem:reg-local-energy} and carries out the every-time suitable-limit passage in Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-limit}.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection*{Lemarié--Rieusset}

\begin{enumerate}
\item Theorem~13.6, printed p.~416, calls for a nonnegative \(\phi\in\mathcal D'\); a distribution has no pointwise nonnegative test-function value. The intended test appears to be a nonnegative compactly supported smooth function. The pressure passage through the nonlocal Riesz operator is compressed into “Similarly.” This theorem serves only for comparison and is not used for the pressure of Definition~\ref{def:riesz-pressure}.

\item Theorem~12.2, printed pp.~352--355, spatial-mollification argument serves only for comparison; it is not a pressure or suitability proof. The manuscript supplies its pressure extension in Theorem~\ref{thm:assoc-pressure} and carries out the suitable Leray limit in Proposition~\ref{prop:leray-limit}.
\end{enumerate}
